Even once these buttons are wired, when my SEMrush/Profound sync no-ops I'd see a cryptic snake_case token instead of plain English telling me what to do next (e.g. set a domain). That's the jargon-leak that erodes my confidence in whether a source is actually working.
- *Grounding:* src/app/(shell)/settings/connectors/actions.ts
- *Fix:* Map each skip reason to a customer sentence ('Add your site domain under Config before syncing SEMrush'), instead of echoing the raw reason code.

**209. ▫︎ 'Each source updates independently. Connectors are additive' tells me nothing about whether anything is actually flowing**
- *Surface:* Connectors intro copy
- *Why it's bad:* The top-of-page note (connectors-client.tsx lines 530-531) and footer repeat 'Connectors are additive — they do not replace manual import' twice, but never answer my actual question: is data coming in, from where, how fresh, and what do I click to refresh. It's reassuring filler in the spot where I most need an at-a-glance 'all 6 connected, last data 2h ago' status summary.
- *Grounding:* src/app/(shell)/settings/connectors/connectors-client.tsx
- *Fix:* Replace the additive boilerplate with a real summary strip: count connected, oldest data age, and a single Refresh button.

**210. ▫︎ GA4 connector reads property metadata only — no sessions, conversions, or landing-page traffic actually used**
- *Surface:* GA4 connector
- *Why it's bad:* The GA4 card and code (connectors-client.tsx comment line 718-720) say 'OAuth + property picker only. NO Data API in this slice — sessions / events / conversions land in Slice 9.A2.' So connecting GA4 picks a property and pulls nothing useful. As an SEO I connect GA4 to tie rankings to traffic and conversions; here it's a glorified property selector that doesn't feed the ranking analysis at all. I assumed connecting it meant my organic-traffic data was in play. It isn't.
- *Grounding:* /Users/armeen/beacon/.claude/worktrees/objective-davinci-c81e70/src/app/(shell)/settings/connectors/connectors-client.tsx
- *Fix:* Either ship the GA4 Data API pull (organic sessions/conversions per landing page, feeding the priority score) or label the GA4 card as 'metadata only — traffic data coming soon' so I'm not misled into thinking my analytics are connected.

**211. ▫︎ Only the trailing 90 days of GSC data is used — no year-over-year or seasonal comparison**
- *Surface:* GSC connector data scope
- *Why it's bad:* gsc-page-signals.ts hard-caps the window at WINDOW_DAYS=90 and the sync 'backfills up to ~90 days.' GSC retains 16 months. Traditional SEO leans heavily on YoY comparisons (was this query seasonal? is the dip real or last-spring-was-just-higher?). Capping at 90 days means I can never see whether a 'decay' is a seasonal pattern or a real decline — I'd flag a normal seasonal dip as a problem and waste a refresh on it.
- *Grounding:* /Users/armeen/beacon/.claude/worktrees/objective-davinci-c81e70/src/domains/recommendation-intelligence/gsc-page-signals.ts
- *Fix:* Backfill and retain the longer GSC history (up to 16 months) and offer a YoY/previous-period comparison so decay detection can distinguish seasonality from genuine decline.

**212. ▫︎ When a day's GSC pull hits quota/network failure, the run stops and returns synced:true with a partial day count — looks like full success**
- *Surface:* GSC sync — quota/network mid-run
- *Why it's bad:* In sync-search-analytics.ts, if pullDayRows returns null (quota/network), the loop just breaks (line 215) and the function returns {synced:true, days, rows_upserted} with whatever it got. So a run that bailed at day 12 of 45 due to a 429 reports synced:true. The summarizer shows 'Synced 12 days' and I have no idea the other 33 days silently failed and need another click. The auth-failure case was fixed to return synced:false (good), but the quota/network case still masquerades as success.
- *Grounding:* src/lib/connectors/gsc/sync-search-analytics.ts
- *Fix:* Track whether the run terminated early on quota/network and surface a 'partial — N of M days, click again to continue' signal rather than an unqualified synced:true.

**213. ▫︎ OAuth error 'not_authorized' has no friendly mapping — I get the raw code**
- *Surface:* /settings/connectors error banner
- *Why it's bad:* The callback can redirect with ?error=not_authorized (when membership check fails), but ERROR_MESSAGES in connectors-client.tsx has no entry for it. The fallback is `Connection error: ${err}` → I literally see 'Connection error: not_authorized.' Same for any future code. For someone who hits error states constantly, raw enum codes are useless and alarming.
- *Grounding:* src/app/(shell)/settings/connectors/connectors-client.tsx
- *Fix:* Add a friendly ERROR_MESSAGES entry for not_authorized (and audit the callback's full set of error codes against the map so none fall through to the raw code).

**214. ▫︎ Disconnecting SEMrush/Profound/Clarity is a HARD delete of the token while GSC/GA4 are soft — inconsistent and lossy**
- *Surface:* SEMrush / Profound / Clarity disconnect
- *Why it's bad:* disconnectSemrush/disconnectProfound/disconnectClarity all call deleteConnectorToken (hard delete), while GSC uses softDisconnectGsc and GA4 sets disconnected_at to preserve cached state + show the 'last refreshed X days ago' copy. So if I disconnect to rotate a key, GSC/GA4 keep my history visibility but SEMrush/Profound/Clarity wipe the token row entirely with no stale-copy affordance. Worse, handleSimpleDisconnect doesn't even setError on failure (lines 438-453) — a failed disconnect is totally silent.
- *Grounding:* src/app/(shell)/settings/connectors/connectors-client.tsx
- *Fix:* Make SEMrush/Profound/Clarity disconnect soft (set disconnected_at) for consistency with GSC/GA4, and surface errors in handleSimpleDisconnect/handleSaveSimpleConnection (both currently ignore result.error).

**215. ▫︎ If saving my SEMrush/Profound/Clarity key fails, the UI shows nothing and I think it worked**
- *Surface:* handleSaveSimpleConnection (SEMrush/Profound/Clarity connect)
- *Why it's bad:* handleSaveSimpleConnection (lines 419-436) awaits the save and on success flips the card to connected — but the else branch is EMPTY. saveSemrushConnection etc. return {success:false, error} on failure (e.g. DB write error), and the client discards it: no setError, no toast, the card just stays on the input form. I paste my key, click Connect, and if it failed I get zero feedback — I'll assume it's connected and wonder for weeks why no data shows.
- *Grounding:* src/app/(shell)/settings/connectors/connectors-client.tsx
- *Fix:* Add an else branch in handleSaveSimpleConnection that calls setError(result.error ?? 'Could not save the connection.').

**216. ▫︎ Connector priority copy buries the fact that NONE of this runs on its own**
- *Surface:* /settings/connectors → /settings/import (Wix self-serve)
- *Why it's bad:* The connectors header (connectors/page.tsx line 61) lists six evidence layers in priority order and says 'GA4... SEMrush... Profound... Clarity add supporting evidence as they connect' — language that strongly implies that once connected, they just work and feed the system. Nowhere on the connectors page does it tell me the truth that the methodology FAQ states plainly: 'There is no background schedule, polling, or SLA' and data only enters 'when you run Sync now'. So the single most important fact for a set-and-forget buyer — that I must manually pull each source — is absent from the exact page where I make connection decisions.
- *Fix:* Add a clear, persistent note at the top of /settings/connectors: 'Connecting a source stores access only. Beacon pulls data when you click Sync now on each card — there is no automatic schedule yet.' Match the honesty of the methodology FAQ at the point of decision.

**217. ▫︎ Disabled Connect buttons give no programmatic reason**
- *Surface:* /settings/connectors
- *Why it's bad:* The 'Connect Wix' button is disabled until both fields are filled, but there's no aria-describedby explaining why. A disabled button with no announced reason just reads 'Connect Wix, dimmed/unavailable' and I have no idea what I missed. Sighted users at least see two empty boxes; I get silence.
- *Grounding:* src/app/(shell)/settings/connectors/connectors-client.tsx (e.g. line 1030 disabled until both Wix fields non-empty; line 1113 Semrush)
- *Fix:* Add aria-describedby pointing to a hint like 'Enter both the API key and Site ID to enable', or keep the button enabled and surface a validation error on click.


## Wix publish / push-live (45)

**218. ⛔ Recommendation drafting is OFF by default (deterministic), so most action types produce no actual page edit to approve**
- *Surface:* LLM drafts — src/lib/llm/config.ts
- *Why it's bad:* config.ts line 17 + 53: default provider is 'deterministic' unless BEACON_LLM_PROVIDER=openai is set with an OPENAI_API_KEY. Per the ground-truth, only ~3 of ~37 action types draft real edits without the LLM on. So when I connect everything and (somehow) get recs, most of them won't even have a concrete 'here's the exact change' — there's nothing to auto-push. The 'specific page edits' that justify the price mostly don't draft.
- *Grounding:* /Users/armeen/beacon/.claude/worktrees/objective-davinci-c81e70/src/lib/llm/config.ts
- *Fix:* For a paying tenant, default to the LLM provider (with the budget caps already in place) or clearly gate the feature behind a setting I control — don't silently ship a degraded deterministic mode that produces empty edits.

**219. ⛔ The 'push approved edits to my Wix site' surface lives under operator-only /diagnostics and 404s for a normal customer**
- *Surface:* /diagnostics/wix publishing (the actual push surface) — operator-gated
- *Why it's bad:* Per the product map, Wix publishing (mapping collections, syncing URLs, the daily-cap push) lives at /diagnostics/wix, which is operator-gated and returns notFound() for a normal customer. The whole reason I connected Wix was for Beacon to push approved SEO edits live. On my phone, as the actual shop owner/customer, the surface that does that is invisible to me. Combined with no autopilot, the 'pushes approved edits live to your Wix site' promise is functionally unreachable.
- *Grounding:* ABSENCE: customer-facing Wix publish surface; the only one is operator-gated src/app/(shell)/diagnostics/wix/page.tsx
- *Fix:* Expose a customer-safe Wix publishing/status surface in the normal shell (not /diagnostics), with the per-edit approve→push flow reachable from the mobile nav.

**220. ⛔ The ONLY button that publishes to my live Wix site lives on an operator-only page that 404s for me**
- *Surface:* /diagnostics/wix · Approve & Push
- *Why it's bad:* I connected Wix expecting to review-and-publish edits. But approveAndPushRecommendedEdit (recommendations/actions.ts:820) is rendered in exactly ONE place: src/app/(shell)/diagnostics/wix/page.tsx, which calls notFound() unless canPublishForCurrentTenant() passes AND the page header literally says 'Operator-mode only.' The customer-facing /recommendations and /recommendations/[id] have NO push button at all. So either I can't publish anything (and Beacon is a glorified copy-paste tool), or the only publish UI is a raw developer-diagnostics screen full of jargon. There is no real, safe, customer-grade 'publish' surface for my livelihood.
- *Grounding:* src/app/(shell)/diagnostics/wix/page.tsx
- *Fix:* Build a first-class customer Approve & Push affordance on /recommendations/[id] with the safety rails (preview, diff, confirm) — do not leave live publishing stranded on a /diagnostics/* page that is by design hidden from the account owner.

**221. ⛔ One bare-form click publishes straight to my live site — no confirmation dialog, no 'are you sure'**
- *Surface:* Approve & Push button
- *Why it's bad:* On /diagnostics/wix the Approve & Push button is a plain <form action={approveAndPushFromForm}> with a single submit (page.tsx:185-190). One click and executePush writes to my real Wix CMS item immediately — no 'This will go live on www.mysite.com — confirm?' modal, no typed confirmation, nothing. For a guy whose site is his income, an accidental click on the wrong card edits the live page instantly. That alone makes me never touch it.
- *Grounding:* src/app/(shell)/diagnostics/wix/page.tsx
- *Fix:* Gate the live write behind an explicit confirmation step that names the exact URL and field about to change, and require a deliberate second action (modal confirm or hold-to-publish) before executePush fires.

**222. ⛔ My 'undo' safety net is stored in ephemeral .data JSON that vanishes on Vercel — so after a cold start there is NO rollback**
- *Surface:* Approve & Push (push-snapshots persistence)
- *Why it's bad:* push-snapshots.ts captures the pre-push value via appendPushSnapshot, which only calls writeStore('push-snapshots'). json-store.ts:149 skips disk entirely when VERCEL=1 (in-process cache only), and there is NO Supabase mirror for push-snapshots (absent from dual-write.ts and supabase/migrations). So in production the snapshot survives only as long as one warm lambda. After the lambda recycles, findLatestSnapshotForEdit returns null and revertPushFromForm silently returns — my pre-push content is gone forever. The whole premise of 'we always have an undo' is false on the real hosting.
- *Grounding:* src/domains/push/push-snapshots.ts
- *Fix:* Persist push-snapshots (and push-ledger) to Supabase with a real migration + dual-write so the pre-push value and the undo are durable across lambda restarts; until then the push path should refuse to write because the fail-closed snapshot is not actually durable.

**223. ⛔ The '10 pushes/day' safety cap silently resets to zero on every Vercel cold start**
- *Surface:* Daily push cap
- *Why it's bad:* checkDailyPushCap (caps.ts:57) counts today's pushes from readPushLedger, but the ledger is written via writeStore('push-ledger') which no-ops to disk on Vercel (json-store.ts:149) with no Supabase mirror. After a cold start the ledger reads empty, so the daily-cap counter resets to 0. The advertised protection ('capped at 10/day') quietly stops protecting me — a runaway batch or repeated clicks could push far more than 10 live edits in a day. A cap I'm told to trust but that doesn't survive a deploy is worse than no cap.
- *Grounding:* src/domains/push/caps.ts
- *Fix:* Back the push ledger with durable Supabase storage so the daily cap is counted from a source that survives restarts; treat a ledger-read failure as cap-exceeded (fail-closed), not cap-empty.

**224. ⛔ A schema (JSON-LD) push can NEVER be reverted — the Revert button refuses every time**
- *Surface:* Revert (schema/seoData pushes)
- *Why it's bad:* buildRevertEdit (push-snapshots.ts:71) clones the original edit, keeping original.action_type='add_schema', and sets proposed_text to the snapshot's previous_text (the OLD seoData JSON string). On revert, executePush re-routes by action_type==='add_schema' (push-service.ts:278) and runs extractJsonLdScriptBlocks() on that raw JSON — which finds zero <script type=...ld+json> blocks → returns 'add_schema card has no extractable JSON-LD block' and refuses. So every structured-data push I make to a live product page is one-way: there is no working undo for it at all.
- *Grounding:* src/domains/push/push-snapshots.ts
- *Fix:* Make revert route by the snapshot's field/adapter target (seoData) and restore the captured seoData tags directly via wixUpdateProductSeoData, rather than re-running the JSON-LD-extraction add_schema path on a value that isn't a script block.

**225. ⛔ There is NO before/after diff at the publish moment — I push blind, seeing only 160 chars of 'proposed' and never the current live text**
- *Surface:* Approve & Push card list
- *Why it's bad:* The pushable-card list (diagnostics/wix/page.tsx:181-194) shows only the URL, field name, and proposed.slice(0,160) — it never fetches or shows the CURRENT live value of that field. So at the exact moment I'm about to overwrite my live page I cannot see what I'm replacing. executePush even has a full dryRun mode (push-service.ts:147) that runs every guard and reports 'what would land' without writing — but it is invoked NOWHERE in the app. The safest possible preview already exists in code and is simply not wired to any button.
- *Grounding:* src/app/(shell)/diagnostics/wix/page.tsx
- *Fix:* Before the live write, call executePush({dryRun:true}) and render a true side-by-side current-vs-proposed diff for the exact field/URL, so I approve a concrete change I can see — not a truncated snippet.

**226. ⛔ The push ledger that's supposed to be my 'what did Beacon change on my live site' record is ephemeral and capped at 1000 rows**
- *Surface:* Push ledger (audit trail durability)
- *Why it's bad:* The push ledger is my only record of what was published to my livelihood's site, but appendPushLedger writes to .data only (no Supabase mirror), so on Vercel it's in-process-cache-only and lost on cold start (json-store.ts:149). It's also slice(-1000) capped (caps.ts:51). So my live-change audit trail can silently disappear after a deploy/restart, and older history is dropped. If something breaks on my site months later I have no durable trail of what Beacon touched and when.
- *Grounding:* src/domains/push/caps.ts
- *Fix:* Persist the push ledger durably to Supabase as an append-only audit log (no aggressive truncation), and surface it as a customer-facing 'changes published to your site' history page.

**227. ⛔ Approve & Push can INSERT a brand-new live page into my Wix CMS with no preview of the page and no easy undo**
- *Surface:* Create-new-page push
- *Why it's bad:* The create:<collectionId> route (push-service.ts:225-267) calls wixInsertDataItem to create a NEW live CMS item from proposed_text parsed as arbitrary JSON fields — including the slug. There's no rendered preview of what that page will look like, no diff (it's net-new), and the snapshot/revert system only handles field UPDATES, not deletions — so removing a wrongly-created page is 'do it by hand in Wix.' Creating a live, indexable page on my site from a JSON blob I never saw rendered, with no in-product delete/undo, is exactly the kind of irreversible action that makes me never trust the publish button.
- *Grounding:* src/domains/push/push-service.ts
- *Fix:* Require a full rendered preview of any net-new page before insert, and provide a one-click 'remove this Beacon-created page' (delete or unpublish) so creation is as reversible as it is easy.

**228. ⛔ "Proof" requires 2-3 comparable unchanged pages with citation history — my small Wix site will never qualify**
- *Surface:* Proof Engine (causal proof)
- *Why it's bad:* The only claim the product calls 'proof' needs minControlsForComputed=2 (and high-confidence needs 3 plus a placebo test) — comparable UNTREATED pages that AI already cites. A typical small business site has a handful of cited pages. With too few controls every change returns weak_estimate/no_controls/insufficient_baseline = 'Still measuring — Beacon can't yet prove whether this change moved your AI citations.' So I pay forever and the 'proof' rail stays empty forever.
- *Grounding:* src/domains/attribution/natural-controls.ts
- *Fix:* Provide an honest secondary proof path for small sites (e.g. before/after conversions + leads on the changed page itself, with clear caveats) instead of gating ALL proof behind a control cohort most SMB sites can't form.

**229. ⛔ I can never push an approved edit to Wix from the customer UI — Approve & Push exists only on an operator 404 page**
- *Surface:* /recommendations (customer V2 queue) → Wix push
- *Why it's bad:* The Wix card promises 'Approved edits can now publish to your site.' But approveAndPushRecommendedEdit is imported ONLY in src/app/(shell)/diagnostics/wix/actions.ts (operator-only, 404 for me). The customer V2 client (recommendations-v2-client.tsx) has a single inline 'Accept' that just calls acceptRecommendation (stamps a changelog) — no push button anywhere. So I connect Wix, approve edits, and nothing ever reaches my live site. The headline feature I bought is unreachable from my account.
- *Grounding:* src/app/(shell)/recommendations/recommendations-v2-client.tsx
- *Fix:* Wire approveAndPushRecommendedEdit into the customer recommendations card behind the existing canPublishForCurrentTenant() gate (which already allows owner/admin members), with clear pushed/verified_live/refused result rendering.

**230. ⛔ The push-to-live path runs ZERO content-safety scan before writing to my Wix site**
- *Surface:* Approve & Push (/recommendations → push-service.ts)
- *Why it's bad:* executePush (src/domains/push/push-service.ts) gates on caps, non-destructive patch, snapshot/undo — but never calls auditRecommendedEditRow or checkCopyDisplaySafe. So a draft containing a hallucinated claim, a competitor's name, a placeholder, or a leftover internal token gets written straight to my live customer-facing page the moment I click Approve & Push. The one scanner that catches exactly these (safety-audit.ts) is wired ONLY to an operator-only diagnostic page that 404s for me. The guardrail exists and is deliberately kept away from the thing that publishes.
- *Grounding:* src/domains/push/push-service.ts
- *Fix:* Call auditRecommendedEditRow inside executePush before any adapter write. On any high-severity violation (competitor_name, placeholder, uuid_leak) hard-refuse with kind:'refused' and the violation list; on medium violations require an explicit per-card override. Make the safety scan a publish-path invariant, not a diagnostic.

**231. ⛔ The 'Proposed' copy I'm about to publish is rendered raw, with the copy-safety display guard never called anywhere in the UI**
- *Surface:* /recommendations drawer — Exact recommended change
- *Why it's bad:* checkCopyDisplaySafe / suggested-copy-display-guard.ts is designed to catch UUIDs, internal tokens (proposed_text, action_type), snake_case and camelCase identifiers leaking into customer copy — but grep shows it is imported by ZERO files under src/app or src/components. The legacy drawer renders d.proposedText directly in a <pre> (recommendations-client.tsx line 1397-1399). Only the 'why' field gets checkWhyDisplaySafe (line 1455). So the literal text I copy-paste onto my site could contain internal junk and the product shows it to me as the recommended change with no guard.
- *Grounding:* src/app/(shell)/recommendations/recommendations-client.tsx
- *Fix:* Wrap the proposedText render in checkCopyDisplaySafe in both recommendations-client.tsx (line ~1397) and the v2 card; on !safe show the calm fallback instead of the raw draft, exactly as why-display-guard already does for 'why'.

**232. ⛔ One click publishes to my live site — no confirmation, no preview, no 'are you sure'**
- *Surface:* Approve & Push (recommendations actions)
- *Why it's bad:* approveAndPushRecommendedEdit (actions.ts line 820) checks publish auth, then immediately calls executePush which writes to Wix; there is no confirmation step, no diff preview, no dry-run gate in the action. A dryRun path exists in push-service but the customer approve action never uses it. As someone terrified of publishing something wrong, a single mis-click on the wrong card mutates my real customer-facing page with no speed bump.
- *Grounding:* src/app/(shell)/recommendations/actions.ts
- *Fix:* Make the customer Approve flow two-step: first call executePush with dryRun:true and show the exact field + before/after that would change, then require a second explicit confirm to do the real write.

**233. ⛔ A tool that generates whole new pages of LLM content to publish exists, and its hallucination guard is just a per-run banned-word list I'd have to hand-author**
- *Surface:* /diagnostics/factory (Page factory)
- *Why it's bad:* factory/page.tsx generates a CLUSTER of brand-new pages from an LLM and lands create_page cards in the same approve queue. Its only content guard is flaggedTerms in the pasted plan ('Farsi' in the example) — a manual banned-word list. There is no factual-accuracy check; the page literally says 'the factory never fabricates deterministic content' but when the LLM IS on it fabricates whole pages whose only safety net is words I remembered to ban. For a worrier this is the nightmare: bulk auto-generated pages about my business, gated by my ability to pre-list every wrong word.
- *Grounding:* src/app/(shell)/diagnostics/factory/page.tsx
- *Fix:* Before factory drafts enter the approve queue, run them through validateSpecificEdit + the safety-audit scanner and a brand-assertion grounding check; reject/quarantine any page making an unsupported claim, not just ones containing a pre-listed banned word.

**234. ⛔ Approve & Push is one full-page-reload form submit per card — no bulk push, no multi-select**
- *Surface:* /diagnostics/wix (publishing)
- *Why it's bad:* wix/page.tsx renders each pushable card with its own <form action={approveAndPushFromForm}> + submit button (lines 181-196), and explicitly states 'Each click publishes ONE card (Invariant 1)' (line 199). To push 10 approved edits live I submit 10 separate forms, each a server round-trip + navigation. There's a daily cap of MAX_PUSHES_PER_DAY but no way to fire them efficiently. For someone who wanted set-and-forget for 6 months, manually clicking publish on each edit forever is the opposite of automation.
- *Grounding:* src/app/(shell)/diagnostics/wix/page.tsx
- *Fix:* Add a multi-select + 'Approve & Push selected (≤cap)' batch action (you already have a batch precedent: acceptAllQueuedFromForm). Respect the per-day cap inside the batch. Even better: an opt-in auto-push policy for high-confidence field edits.

**235. ⚠️ The active site is shown only as a faint inline pill — easy to push edits to the WRONG client**
- *Surface:* Tenant switcher visibility
- *Why it's bad:* The only indication of which client I'm operating on is a low-contrast pill in the header (`text-[12px]`, muted border, tenant-switcher.tsx lines 57-61). There's no persistent site name in the sidebar, no color coding, no confirmation on destructive/publish actions naming the tenant. With 12 near-identically-laid-out dashboards and Approve & Push writing live to a client's Wix site, mistaking which tenant I'm on is a real money/reputation risk.
- *Grounding:* src/components/shell/tenant-switcher.tsx
- *Fix:* Make the active tenant unmistakable (sidebar header, color accent) and name the tenant explicitly in the Approve & Push confirmation.

**236. ⚠️ A refused or capped Revert fails completely silently — I'd think my site was restored when it wasn't**
- *Surface:* Revert button
- *Why it's bad:* revertPushFromForm (diagnostics/wix/actions.ts:170) returns void and, when the revert is refused (daily cap hit, non-destructive guard, empty previous value), it only does console.warn on the server. The form gives me zero on-screen feedback. So I click 'Revert', the page revalidates, the ledger shows nothing changed, and I have no idea whether my live page was actually rolled back. For an undo action that's the scariest possible failure mode — believing you're safe when you aren't.
- *Grounding:* src/app/(shell)/diagnostics/wix/actions.ts
- *Fix:* Convert revert to a server action that returns a typed result and render an explicit on-page outcome ('Reverted', or 'Revert blocked: daily cap reached — try tomorrow'), never a silent no-op.

**237. ⚠️ The real publish flow is 'copy this text and paste it into Wix yourself' — the automation I paid for is a clipboard button**
- *Surface:* Suggested copy (Act 4, /recommendations/[id])
- *Why it's bad:* The customer-facing Suggested Copy act (suggested-copy-act.tsx) renders a CopyButton + a Whereline that literally says 'Add this title tag to [page].' That's it — copy to clipboard, then go hand-edit Wix myself. I connected Wix specifically so I wouldn't have to do that. For most edits there is no in-product push; the headline 'approved edits publish directly to your site' collapses into manual paste work. I'm doing the labor and Beacon is taking credit for 'automation.'
- *Grounding:* src/app/(shell)/recommendations/[id]/suggested-copy-act.tsx
- *Fix:* Wire the customer brief's Suggested Copy to the same safe push path (preview → confirm → publish → verify) for Wix-mapped pages, so 'copy/paste into Wix' is the fallback, not the default experience.

**238. ⚠️ Most of my edits aren't pushable at all — they silently fall through to 'paste-ready' with no clear reason on the card**
- *Surface:* Approve & Push eligibility
- *Why it's bad:* executePush only writes for three narrow shapes: create:<collection>, add_schema→Stores product seoData, and field:<itemField> (push-service.ts). CMS dynamic pages have no per-item SEO field, so add_schema cards refuse; any card whose target_element_key isn't field:/create: refuses with 'export it as a dev note.' The customer pushable list on /diagnostics/wix only shows field-targeted, URL-in-map cards — everything else just isn't there. I have a queue of recommendations and no way to tell which ones can ever go live vs which are dead-on-arrival paste work, until I dig into operator screens.
- *Grounding:* src/domains/push/push-service.ts
- *Fix:* On each recommendation, show an honest, plain-English publishability badge ('Beacon can publish this to Wix' vs 'Manual paste — Wix can't take this field via API') derived from the same routing logic, so I know up front what's actually automatable.

**239. ⚠️ After publishing I get 'pushed · probe pending' with no proof my live page actually changed and no diff of what changed**
- *Surface:* Approve & Push (post-push verification)
- *Why it's bad:* approveAndPushRecommendedEdit (recommendations/actions.ts:860-885) fires probeLiveText, a single 15s prefix-match fetch of the raw HTML. If it doesn't match (very common for JS-rendered Wix pages), I just get '· probe pending (scan cadence will verify)'. There's no rendered confirmation of the actual before→after on my live page, no link to view it, no 'here is exactly the field that changed.' I changed my livelihood's page and Beacon shrugs 'we'll check later.' I have to go open Wix and eyeball it myself to trust anything happened.
- *Grounding:* src/app/(shell)/recommendations/actions.ts
- *Fix:* After a successful write, show a concrete what-changed record (field, old value, new value, target URL with a 'view live' link) and a real verification state — not just an optimistic 'pushed' with a maybe-probe.

**240. ⚠️ The 'won't blank your content' guard is a crude 80%-shrink heuristic that both over- and under-protects**
- *Surface:* Non-destructive guard
- *Why it's bad:* assertNonDestructivePatch (caps.ts:83) only refuses if proposed is empty OR (current>80 chars AND proposed<20% of current length). So replacing a 500-char paragraph with 120 chars of completely different/wrong text sails right through (120 > 20% of 500), overwriting good content live. Meanwhile a legitimate tightening of a long blurb to a punchy short line gets blocked as 'deletion-shaped.' It's a length ratio, not a content check — it gives me false confidence that 'Beacon won't destroy my content' while letting real content loss through.
- *Grounding:* src/domains/push/caps.ts
- *Fix:* Replace the length-ratio heuristic with a content-aware check (e.g., require the proposed text to be shown in a confirmed diff, and treat large semantic replacements as requiring explicit confirmation regardless of length), and stop marking reasonable shortenings as destructive.

**241. ⚠️ If a field was empty before the push, there is NO way to undo — the page now has Beacon's text stuck on it**
- *Surface:* Revert (empty previous value)
- *Why it's bad:* buildRevertEdit refuses when snapshot.previous_text is empty (push-snapshots.ts:76) because restoring an empty value is 'deletion-shaped' and 'do that one by hand.' So if Beacon ADDS a meta description / FAQ / heading to a field that was previously blank, and I then want it gone, the Revert button can't help me — it just won't build. I'm told to 'do that one by hand' in Wix, which for a non-technical owner who was promised automated rollback is exactly the dead end I feared.
- *Grounding:* src/domains/push/push-snapshots.ts
- *Fix:* Support reverting an additive push back to empty via an explicit, clearly-labeled 'remove what Beacon added' confirmation, instead of refusing and dumping the cleanup on me.

**242. ⚠️ To publish content edits I must hand-paste a raw JSON array of collection/field mappings into a textarea — there is no normal setup**
- *Surface:* Wix collection mapping setup
- *Why it's bad:* saveWixMappings (diagnostics/wix/actions.ts:51) expects me to paste a JSON array of {dataCollectionId, slugField, urlPrefix, contentFieldRoles...} into a <textarea> on the operator page (page.tsx:154-164). Without correct contentFieldRoles mappings, content edits silently stay 'paste-ready' and never publish (push-service.ts:218-224). A Wix site owner has no idea what his dataCollectionId or slugField are, and certainly won't author JSON. So 'connect Wix and approved edits publish directly' is gated behind a developer-only JSON config step that I can neither see (404) nor complete.
- *Grounding:* src/app/(shell)/diagnostics/wix/actions.ts
- *Fix:* Replace the raw-JSON paste with a guided mapper that reads my Wix collections via the API and lets me pick which field is the title/H1/meta in a dropdown UI on a customer-reachable page.

**243. ⚠️ No way to approve-and-publish a batch — even after reviewing 15 good edits I must click Push 15 separate times**
- *Surface:* Bulk publishing
- *Why it's bad:* acceptAllQueuedFromForm flips up to 20 cards to 'accepted' but explicitly 'never publishes' (diagnostics/wix/actions.ts:206-227). The only publish path is one <form> per card. So even when I've carefully reviewed a stack of safe edits and trust them, I can't say 'publish these 8' — it's one manual click per edit, on a hidden page. That's not just friction; it guarantees I'll never keep up, and the queue will rot.
- *Grounding:* src/app/(shell)/diagnostics/wix/actions.ts
- *Fix:* Add a reviewed-batch publish (select N edits → preview the combined diff → confirm once → publish with per-edit results and a single undo-all), keeping per-edit safety but removing the one-click-per-card tax.

**244. ⚠️ Nothing stops me from pushing the same edit twice and burning two of my 10 daily pushes on one change**
- *Surface:* Approve & Push (idempotency / double-click)
- *Why it's bad:* executePush's status guard allows pushing from recommended/accepted/push_failed (push-service.ts:113) but a successfully 'pushed' edit's re-push protection depends on the persistence flip happening; the bare <form> submit has no client-side disable/dedupe, and the cap counts every successful push (caps.ts:65). A double submit (slow network, impatient click) can write the same field twice and consume two of my precious 10/day cap slots, or re-apply an edit I already reverted. For a cap I'm told to rely on, accidental double-spend is a real risk.
- *Grounding:* src/domains/push/push-service.ts
- *Fix:* Make the push action idempotent per edit+content-hash (refuse a re-push that matches the last successful push), and disable the button immediately on submit with a pending state.

**245. ⚠️ Most action types produce no real edit to publish — so 'Approve & Push' has almost nothing to act on**
- *Surface:* Recommendation drafts (deterministic default)
- *Why it's bad:* Per the ground-truth state, LLM drafting is off by default (BEACON_LLM_PROVIDER=deterministic) and only ~3 of ~37 action types produce real edits. executePush requires concrete proposed_text/field targets; cards without a drafted edit refuse or stay paste-ready (push-service.ts:218-224, 432-439). So the publish pipeline I'm terrified of is mostly empty: the few things that CAN be pushed are a tiny slice, and the rest are advice. I can't even evaluate the scary publish flow because there's barely anything pushable — which also means the product isn't doing the work.
- *Grounding:* src/domains/push/push-service.ts
- *Fix:* Be explicit in-product about which recommendations have a publishable draft vs advice-only, and turn on grounded drafting for the common action types so the push path has real, reviewable edits to act on.

**246. ⚠️ Undoing a mistake counts against my 10/day push cap — so a bad day can leave me unable to roll back**
- *Surface:* Revert reuses the daily push cap
- *Why it's bad:* The Revert button's title text admits it 'counts against the daily push cap' (diagnostics/wix/page.tsx:235) and revertPushFromForm ships through the same executePush + checkDailyPushCap (actions.ts:195). So if I've used my 10 pushes and then realize several were wrong, I literally cannot undo them today — the cap blocks my rollback. The safety cap actively prevents the safety undo. That's backwards for someone whose priority is reversibility.
- *Grounding:* src/app/(shell)/diagnostics/wix/actions.ts
- *Fix:* Exempt reverts/rollbacks from the daily push cap (or give them a separate higher allowance) so I can always undo a mistake immediately, regardless of how many forward pushes I've made.

**247. ⚠️ There is no customer-facing 'here is exactly what Beacon changed on your live site, when, and the old value' view**
- *Surface:* What-actually-changed history
- *Why it's bad:* The only place that shows pushes is the operator push-ledger section on /diagnostics/wix (page.tsx:216-254), which I can't reach, and even that shows just result + url + a one-line detail string — not the actual old→new content. /changes tracks recommendation lifecycle, not the literal live-site field writes with their pre-push values. So after Beacon edits my site I have no trustworthy, readable record of what was altered and what it used to say. That uncertainty is precisely what stops me from using it.
- *Grounding:* ABSENCE: no customer-reachable per-field old→new push history exists; the push ledger lives only on operator /diagnostics/wix (src/app/(shell)/diagnostics/wix/page.tsx:216) with no before/after values
- *Fix:* Build a customer-facing 'Published to your site' log (per push: URL, field, old value, new value, timestamp, revert button) backed by durable storage, so I always know exactly what changed and can undo it.

**248. ⚠️ Adding a prompt forces me to fill a required 'Topic id' slug field — internal taxonomy bleeding into my workflow**
- *Surface:* /settings/prompts (Add prompt form)
- *Why it's bad:* settings-prompts-client.tsx line 132: the 'Topic id' input is required, with placeholder 'e.g. [city] [service]'. I just want to track the question 'What's the best X for Y?' — I shouldn't have to invent and maintain an internal topic-id slug to do it. It's a data-model field exposed as a mandatory user input. This is operator/DB plumbing pushed onto me.
- *Grounding:* src/app/(shell)/settings/prompts/settings-prompts-client.tsx
- *Fix:* Auto-derive topic_id from the prompt text server-side (or make it optional with a sensible default). Don't require me to author internal taxonomy keys.

**249. ⚠️ A real Wix write FAILURE (429/500) is reported to me as 'refused' — the same word used for 'I'm protecting you'**
- *Surface:* Wix push (executePush write failure)
- *Why it's bad:* In push-service.ts, when the actual Wix API write fails (wixUpdateDataItem/wixUpdateProductSeoData returns !ok after exhausting retries on 429/502/503), executePush returns {kind:'refused', reason:'wix write failed: api_error (http_429...)'}. The caller approveAndPushRecommendedEdit treats every 'refused' identically — returns {ok:false, reason}. So 'Wix rate-limited you, try again in a minute' looks identical to 'Beacon refused because this would change a URL.' One is recoverable and transient; the other is permanent policy. I can't tell which, so I don't know whether to retry.
- *Grounding:* src/domains/push/push-service.ts
- *Fix:* Add a distinct PushResult kind (e.g. 'push_failed_transient') for API/network/429 write failures vs the policy 'refused', and render a 'Retry' affordance for the transient case with the Retry-After hint.

**250. ⚠️ When a Wix write fails, the edit's lifecycle status is NOT flipped to push_failed — only the invisible ledger records it**
- *Surface:* approveAndPushRecommendedEdit — push_failed not persisted to edit status
- *Why it's bad:* On a successful push, the action calls markRecommendedEditPushResult to persist 'pushed.' But on result.kind==='refused' (which is also where real write failures land), it returns {ok:false} and NEVER calls markRecommendedEditPushResult. The push_failed status is written only inside push-service.recordLedger (observability) — the edit row I see in the queue is never marked failed. So a failed push leaves the card looking exactly like it was never attempted; I can't see 'this one failed, retry it.' PUSHABLE_FROM even includes 'push_failed' for retry, but the status is never set to push_failed from this path, so the retry affordance can't engage.
- *Grounding:* src/app/(shell)/recommendations/actions.ts
- *Fix:* In approveAndPushRecommendedEdit, on a transient/api write failure call markRecommendedEditPushResult with result:'push_failed' so the edit shows a retryable failed state in the queue.

**251. ⚠️ Beacon scrapes my own page text and proposes publishing it back as my meta description — garbage in, garbage out, under Beacon's name**
- *Surface:* draft-enrichment.ts (composeMeta / composeTitle)
- *Why it's bad:* composeMeta builds the proposed description by joining my h1 + h2s + card_texts and clipping to 155 chars (lines 360-392). If my page structure is messy (and the code's own comments admit Wix hides body copy and injects zero-width junk and colon-terminated label fragments), the 'recommended' description is a Frankenstein of heading fragments. It's presented as Beacon's expert suggestion with expected_impact copy claiming it 'lifts click-through'. I'd trust that and publish a broken-reading snippet to Google with my brand on it.
- *Grounding:* src/domains/recommendation-intelligence/draft-enrichment.ts
- *Fix:* Hold meta/title drafts to a readability bar (complete sentence, no fragment joins, min prose length from real body copy only) and otherwise abstain rather than emit a stitched-together draft labeled as an improvement.

**252. ⚠️ Beacon auto-fills my company as the Article author/publisher in JSON-LD from a GUESSED brand name**
- *Surface:* draft-enrichment.ts (composeContentArticleSchema)
- *Why it's bad:* composeContentArticleSchema sets author + publisher Organization name to orgName, which is inferBrandSuffix's GUESS at my brand from the most common ' | Tail' across my titles (lines 82-119, 595-600). The code itself documents this misfires on Wix dynamic pages where the common tail is a collection name, not the business. So the structured data I paste/publish could declare the wrong organization as the author/publisher of my content — a machine-readable false brand assertion served to every search and AI engine.
- *Grounding:* src/domains/recommendation-intelligence/draft-enrichment.ts
- *Fix:* Do not emit author/publisher in schema from an inferred suffix. Only populate Organization name from an operator-confirmed business name (config), and omit it otherwise (the code already omits-when-missing — just stop trusting the inference for an identity claim).

**253. ⚠️ A field push is a full-item PUT read-modify-write — a stale read can clobber other fields on my live page**
- *Surface:* push-service.ts (Wix CMS field write)
- *Why it's bad:* The wix_cms EDIT route does wixQueryDataItems then wixUpdateDataItem as a full-item replace (lines 453-519, comment at 468 confirms 'a Wix item update is a full-item PUT'). It merges only the one field over whatever it just read. If the read is stale or another edit happened between read and write, the PUT writes back stale values for every OTHER field on my CMS item. For me that means approving a meta-description change could silently revert a price, a phone number, or body copy I changed in Wix five minutes ago — a brand-data-integrity hazard with no field-level diff shown to me.
- *Grounding:* src/domains/push/push-service.ts
- *Fix:* Use a field-level/partial update API if Wix supports it, or re-read immediately before write and diff the full item, refusing (or warning) if any non-target field changed since the snapshot.

**254. ⚠️ Undo refuses to restore an empty previous value, so the one case I most need to undo is the one it won't**
- *Surface:* push-service.ts (revert / undo)
- *Why it's bad:* revertPushFromForm/buildRevertEdit (wix/actions.ts 170-204) restore the snapshot's previous_text but the comment notes 'Empty previous values refuse (deletion-shaped restores need a human in Wix).' If Beacon ADDED content to a field that was previously empty (e.g. added a meta description where there was none) and it's wrong/off-brand, the automated revert refuses and tells me to go fix it by hand in Wix. The scariest publish — net-new content under my brand — is exactly the one the undo button abandons.
- *Grounding:* src/app/(shell)/diagnostics/wix/actions.ts
- *Fix:* For empty-previous (additive) pushes, implement undo as 'clear the field back to empty' (the actual prior state) rather than refusing, so every push Beacon makes is one-click reversible.

**255. ⚠️ 'Accept all queued' flips up to 20 cards to accepted in one click with no per-card safety review**
- *Surface:* /diagnostics/wix — Batch accept
- *Why it's bad:* acceptAllQueuedFromForm (wix/actions.ts 212-228) marks up to 20 recommended cards 'accepted' at once. The comment frames it as 'approve the morning ticket stack.' Even though it doesn't push, accepting in bulk means 20 unscanned drafts are staged for a quick Approve & Push with me never having read 19 of them. Set-and-forget plus bulk-accept plus no content scan is precisely how something off-brand slips live without anyone reading it.
- *Grounding:* src/app/(shell)/diagnostics/wix/actions.ts
- *Fix:* Require the safety scan to pass per card before bulk-accept includes it, and exclude any card with a high-severity violation from the batch (or show the count of flagged cards being skipped).

**256. ⚠️ The one batch action that exists ('Accept all queued ≤20') is on an operator-only diagnostics page I can't reach from the nav**
- *Surface:* /diagnostics/wix (publishing)
- *Why it's bad:* wix/page.tsx line 204-212 has 'Accept all queued (≤20) — staging only', the closest thing to a bulk action in the whole app. But /diagnostics/* is operator-gated (404 for normal users) and isn't in navigation.ts or the ⌘K palette. So the only bulk affordance is hidden from the actual user. Meanwhile the customer-facing /recommendations has none.
- *Grounding:* src/app/(shell)/diagnostics/wix/page.tsx
- *Fix:* Move a safe bulk-accept into the customer /recommendations surface. Keep destructive/publish batches gated but expose accept-batch where the work happens.

**257. ▫︎ The config slug placeholder is 'business-name-san-jose' — a Bay Area builder leak in a field I don't understand anyway**
- *Surface:* /settings/config
- *Why it's bad:* Line 163 placeholder 'e.g. business-name-san-jose' bakes in a San Jose (Bay Area) example, a leftover from the builder tenant. Combined with an industry dropdown where 'Home Builder / Contractor' is the very first option (line 8), the settings screen keeps signaling this tool is really for a California contractor, not my bakery. Small thing, but it chips away at my confidence that any of the numbers are tuned for me.
- *Grounding:* src/app/(shell)/settings/config/config-form.tsx
- *Fix:* Use a neutral/industry-appropriate placeholder (or derive from my own business name), and don't lead the industry dropdown with 'Home Builder'.

**258. ▫︎ The 'verified live' badge keys off a fragile 120-char raw-HTML prefix match that will almost always say 'pending' for Wix**
- *Surface:* Approve & Push (probe match logic)
- *Why it's bad:* probeLiveText (push-service.ts:564) fetches raw HTML and does a whitespace-normalized first-120-char includes() check. Wix pages are heavily client-rendered and the edited CMS field often isn't present verbatim in the initial HTML, so the probe will usually return found=false → I get '· probe pending' even when the push genuinely worked. So 'verified live' rarely fires, training me to distrust the one signal that's supposed to tell me my change landed.
- *Grounding:* src/domains/push/push-service.ts
- *Fix:* Either render the page the way a crawler/browser would (or query the Wix API for the item's new value) to verify, and stop presenting a near-always-false prefix probe as the live-verification signal.

**259. ▫︎ Pushes can refuse with cryptic developer errors that I have no way to act on**
- *Surface:* Approve & Push (status preconditions)
- *Why it's bad:* executePush returns refusal reasons like 'no Wix CMS item mapped for <url> — run the url-map sync on /diagnostics/wix first' (push-service.ts:449) and 'card targets "(none)" — export it as a dev note or re-queue with a field target' (push-service.ts:437). These surface to me (the refusal reason bubbles up through approveAndPushRecommendedEdit → ApproveAndPushResult.reason). They reference operator pages I can't open and concepts (url-map sync, field target, dev note) I don't understand. A failed publish leaves me staring at jargon with no next step.
- *Grounding:* src/domains/push/push-service.ts
- *Fix:* Map every push refusal to a plain-English, actionable customer message with a concrete next step I can actually take, and never leak internal route names or schema terms.

**260. ▫︎ The push card never lets me open the actual live page I'm about to edit before I commit**
- *Surface:* Approve & Push (no live page link before publishing)
- *Why it's bad:* The pushable card (diagnostics/wix/page.tsx:181-194) shows the URL as plain mono text, not a clickable link, and offers no 'open this page' affordance next to the Push button. So I can't quickly go look at the real page in context right before approving a change to it. For someone who wants certainty before anything goes live, not even being able to one-click-view the target page is a needless gap.
- *Grounding:* src/app/(shell)/diagnostics/wix/page.tsx
- *Fix:* Make the target URL a real link (open in new tab) right beside the push/preview controls so I can inspect the live page in context immediately before approving.

**261. ▫︎ Revert is also a one-click bare form — I could accidentally roll BACK a good change with no confirm**
- *Surface:* Revert (no confirmation either)
- *Why it's bad:* The Revert control (diagnostics/wix/page.tsx:229-239) is the same pattern as Push: a single <form> submit, no confirmation, no preview of what the field will revert TO. So just as an accidental Push is one click, an accidental Revert is one click — and it ships through executePush, consuming a cap slot and overwriting my current (possibly good) live value with the old snapshot value, all without showing me the diff or asking 'restore <old> over <current>?'.
- *Grounding:* src/app/(shell)/diagnostics/wix/page.tsx
- *Fix:* Give Revert the same preview+confirm treatment as Push: show current-vs-restore diff and require explicit confirmation before it writes the old value back to the live site.

**262. ▫︎ Battlecards add a 'Local pressure / geographic presence' dimension to EVERY competitor — irrelevant for a non-local Wix business**
- *Surface:* /competitors → 'Competitive comparison' battlecards (geographic dimension)
- *Why it's bad:* computeBattlecards always tries to add a geographic_presence dimension based on city coverage (battlecards.ts lines 131-149), and pressure_topics get the ' (Bay Area)' strip in the UI. If I'm a national/online Wix business with no city strategy, my battlecards get cluttered with 'Competitor presence in markets where you're weak: [cities]' — a dimension that doesn't apply to me, derived from a geo model seeded with Bay Area assumptions. It makes the head-to-head read like it's grading me on the wrong game.
- *Grounding:* src/domains/competitors/battlecards.ts
- *Fix:* Gate the geographic dimension behind the tenant's local_service feature flag (the page already checks tenantFeatures.local_service for LocalPressureSection — apply the same gate inside battlecards) so non-local tenants don't get geo dimensions.


## Recommendations / action queue (90)

**263. ⛔ Switching sites always dumps you back to '/' — you lose your place every single time**
- *Surface:* Tenant switcher (app header)
- *Why it's bad:* With 12 client sites I live in the switcher. But switchTenantFromForm ALWAYS ends with `redirect('/')` (tenant-switch-action.ts). If I'm reviewing recommendations for Client A and switch to Client B to compare, I'm thrown back to Today and have to re-navigate to /recommendations. Multiply by 12 sites x every comparison = constant context loss. There's no 'stay on the same page for the new tenant.'
- *Grounding:* src/app/(shell)/tenant-switch-action.ts
- *Fix:* Redirect back to the referring path (or pass current pathname through the form) so switching keeps me on /recommendations, /changes, etc. for the new tenant.

**264. ⛔ No bulk actions — every recommendation is a one-at-a-time Accept, across every site**
- *Surface:* /recommendations
- *Why it's bad:* The recommendations table has search + two filter dropdowns but ZERO row-selection: no checkboxes, no 'select all', no 'Accept all', no bulk dismiss (grep for select/bulk/checkbox in recommendations-client.tsx finds only the filter <select>s and CSS select-none). acceptRecommendation and approveAndPushRecommendedEdit each take a single id. For an agency triaging dozens of recs per site x 12 sites, clicking Accept one card at a time is a non-starter — this is exactly the 'furious at one-at-a-time' wall.
- *Grounding:* src/app/(shell)/recommendations/recommendations-client.tsx
- *Fix:* Add multi-select checkboxes with bulk Accept/Dismiss/Defer (and bulk Approve & Push) on the recommendations table.

**265. ⛔ Default recommendations view runs the full pipeline LIVE on every load — '~30s cold' per the code's own comment**
- *Surface:* /recommendations (legacy mode)
- *Why it's bad:* The page header comment states legacy is the 'full live pipeline (~30 s cold)' and v2 'persisted fast loader (~500 ms cold)' (recommendations/page.tsx lines 95-98), and legacy is the DEFAULT unless BEACON_RECOMMENDATIONS_V2=true or ?v2=1. So unless someone flipped an env flag I'll never see, every visit to my recs queue — for each of 12 sites — can take ~30 seconds to compute on the fly. That is the definition of 'anything slow' that I'm furious about.
- *Grounding:* src/app/(shell)/recommendations/page.tsx
- *Fix:* Make the persisted v2 loader the default (it's already 60x faster); stop recomputing the whole pipeline on every page view.

**266. ⛔ The default recommendations view is a horizontally-scrolling table with a 24px-tall Accept button buried in a 120px column**
- *Surface:* /recommendations (default legacy) — src/app/(shell)/recommendations/recommendations-client.tsx
- *Why it's bad:* page.tsx defaults to the LEGACY table (V2 cards only fire behind BEACON_RECOMMENDATIONS_V2 env). The table at line 451 is `overflow-x-auto` with columns w-[68px]/w-[72px]/w-[110px]/w-[120px], and the Accept/approve button (line 1054) is `text-[11px] px-2 py-1` — roughly 24px tall — wedged into the right-most 120px column that I have to horizontally scroll to reach. Approving an edit between customers is the single most important action I take, and it's the hardest thing to tap in the whole app.
- *Grounding:* src/app/(shell)/recommendations/recommendations-client.tsx
- *Fix:* Default mobile to the V2 card stack (which has full-width tappable cards), or at minimum render the legacy table as stacked cards under `md` with a full-width, 44px-tall Accept button per recommendation.

**267. ⛔ No surface in the app converts a table to cards on small screens — every data-dense view is a desktop table**
- *Surface:* ABSENCE: any responsive/mobile-card layout for data tables
- *Why it's bad:* I grepped the data surfaces: /changes, /recommendations (legacy default), /competitors, /settings/history all render desktop HTML tables or fixed CSS grids. Not one has a `block md:table` / card-stack fallback. The only card layout (V2 recs) is hidden behind an env flag and off by default. As a phone-only user, EVERY place where I'd actually read my results is a table built for a mouse. The product is, in practice, desktop-only for anything beyond the home hero.
- *Grounding:* ABSENCE: no responsive table-to-card pattern exists across src/app/(shell)/changes, /recommendations, /competitors
- *Fix:* Adopt a shared responsive table→card pattern and apply it to /changes, /recommendations, /competitors, /settings/history. Default the V2 card surfaces ON for mobile viewports.

**268. ⛔ There is NO spend dashboard anywhere — the entire cost ledger is invisible to me**
- *Surface:* Whole product / global (no Spend or Billing page exists)
- *Why it's bad:* I am a cost-paranoid bootstrapped founder. Beacon literally meters my spend at $0.005/obs (Perplexity) and $0.012/obs (ChatGPT) and tracks it in cost-ledger.json + llm-budget.json, with helpers getTenantSpendToday / getMonthlySpend / getBudgetState specifically described 'for display in dashboards.' I grepped every file under src/app and src/components: ZERO callers. The dashboard those helpers were written for was never built. I cannot see one dollar of what this thing is spending of my money. That is the single thing I care about most and it does not exist.
- *Grounding:* ABSENCE: no UI calls getTenantSpendToday/getGlobalSpendToday/getMonthlySpend/getBudgetState/checkMonthlyBudget — verified 0 hits across src/app + src/components; helpers live in src/lib/cost/budget.ts (lines 317-328), src/lib/cost/monthly.ts (185-197), src/domains/recommendations/adjudicator-budget.ts (118-120)
- *Fix:* Build a customer-facing Spend page (e.g. /settings/usage) that calls getTenantSpendToday + getMonthlySpend + getBudgetState and renders today's spend, this-month's spend, the cap, and % used. Link it from the Settings tab bar.

**269. ⛔ My spend cap is an environment variable I can never set — there is no UI to set or even see it**
- *Surface:* Budget caps (BEACON_DAILY_BUDGET_USD_PER_TENANT etc.)
- *Why it's bad:* The whole reason I'd trust set-and-forget is a hard ceiling I control. The cap exists ($10/day per tenant, $200/month, $10/month LLM) but it's read exclusively from env vars (BEACON_DAILY_BUDGET_USD_PER_TENANT, BEACON_MONTHLY_BUDGET_USD) or a JSON file. setBudgetCap() exists in code but has zero UI callers. I'm a non-technical founder — I cannot SSH into Vercel to set an env var. So the only thing standing between me and a runaway bill is a number I can neither see nor change inside the app.

**298. ⚠️ Decay uses a 28-day window while low-CTR/striking use 90-day — inconsistent, unexplained windows**
